What brings you in to see me today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 48 / stocker_or_order_filler / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"What scared me enough to come in is that I’ve been hearing my name called from the hallway at home and once at the warehouse when nobody was there, and it sounds as real as my husband standing right next to me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]

At home it’s from the hallway, and once at work too.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually alone when you hear your name being called?\n\nPatient: Usually, yeah. At home nobody’s in the hallway when it happens. It’s just my name, like someone’s calling me from the hall or standing right there. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you see anything when you hear your name being called?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t see anything. I just hear my name, like it’s coming from the hallway or right next to me. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear your name being called?\n\nPatient: Not every day… but enough that it scared me. Mostly at home from the hallway, and once at the warehouse. It’s happened a few times.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear anything else besides your name being called when nobody's there?\n\nPatient: Not really… it’s mostly just my name. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do these voices calling your name cause you any distress or affect your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does bother me. It scared me enough to come in, because it sounds so real—like my husband is right there calling me from the hallway, but nobody’s there. And once it happened at the warehouse too, so… yeah, it’s hard to just ignore.\n\nDoctor: Is it always the same voice that calls your name?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… it usually sounds like my husband, like he’s right there in the hallway. It sounded real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever comment on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: No… not really commenting. It’s more just my name being called, like from the hallway at home. It sounds so real, like my husband is right there, but nobody’s there. It’s just my name, like someone’s calling me from the hall or standing right there. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you see anything when you hear your name being called?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t see anything. I just hear my name, like it’s coming from the hallway or right next to me. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear your name being called?\n\nPatient: Not every day… but enough that it scared me. Mostly at home from the hallway, and once at the warehouse. It’s happened a few times.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear anything else besides your name being called when nobody's there?\n\nPatient: Not really… it’s mostly just my name. Like from the hallway at home, and that one time at work. It sounds real, like my husband is right next to me, but nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I see things. It’s more the hearing my name, like from the hallway at home, or that time at the warehouse. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do these voices calling your name cause you any distress or affect your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does bother me. It scared me enough to come in, because it sounds so real—like my husband is right there calling me from the hallway, but nobody’s there. And once it happened at the warehouse too, so… yeah, it’s hard to just ignore.\n\nDoctor: Is it always the same voice that calls your name?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… it usually sounds like my husband, like he’s right there in the hallway. The time at the warehouse, it was still my name, but I don’t know if I’d say it was exactly the same. It just sounded real.\n\nDoctor: Is the voice that calls your name always the same?\n\nPatient: Not always, I don’t think. At home it sounds like my husband calling me from the hallway… real close, like he’s right there. The warehouse one, I’m not sure whose voice it was, just my name. It sounded real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever comment on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: No… not really commenting. It’s more just my name being called, like from the hallway at home. It sounds so real, like my husband is right there, but nobody’s there. Once it happened at the warehouse too.\n\nDoctor: How long does the voice last when it calls your name?\n\nPatient: Just a second or two, I guess. It’s like I hear my name, clear as if he’s right there, and then it’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice calling your name is coming from inside your head or from outside?\n\nPatient: Outside, I think. Like from the hallway… or at work that one time. It doesn’t feel like it’s in my head. It sounds real, like someone’s right there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your name being called, do you hear it in one ear or both ears?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t notice it in just one ear. It’s more like it’s out in the hallway, like someone really called me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voice calling your name only at specific places like home or the warehouse, or can it happen anywhere?\n\nPatient: Mostly at home, from the hallway. It happened once at the warehouse too, when nobody was around. I don’t know if it could happen anywhere… those are just the places I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever smell or taste anything that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It’s not smells or tastes. It’s more the… hearing my name thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
